# docs/specs/webapp.yaml — General Web App audit rules
version: "1.0"
profile: webapp

rules:
  - id: webapp-001
    profile: webapp
    severity: critical
    title: "No rate limiting on API routes"
    rationale: >
      An API without rate limiting is exposed to abuse and denial-of-service
      pressure. On serverless platforms it can also create direct cost risk. In
      reliability engineering terms, there is no protection for the shared error
      budget.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "rateLimit|rate-limit|upstash|redis|kv"
        must_exist: false
        file_glob: "**/*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}"
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No rate limiting pattern found in application source files"
    fix: |
      Use @upstash/ratelimit with Vercel KV or middleware rate limiting.
    references:
      - book: "Site Reliability Engineering"
        concept: "error-budgets"
      - standard: "OWASP Top 10"
        concept: "api-abuse"

  - id: webapp-002
    profile: webapp
    severity: error
    title: "Missing Content Security Policy"
    rationale: >
      Without CSP, injected JavaScript has a larger attack surface. Hardening
      headers are a relatively cheap deployment-time control because they can be
      applied centrally without changing every application code path.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "Content-Security-Policy|contentSecurityPolicy"
        must_exist: false
        file_glob: "**/*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx,json}"
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No CSP header configuration found"
    fix: |
      Add the Content-Security-Policy header in next.config.js or server header configuration.
    references:
      - book: "Web Application Security"
        concept: "output-encoding"
      - book: "Continuous Delivery"
        concept: "deployment-pipeline"


===== END FILE: docs/specs/webapp.yaml =====

===== FILE: docs/specs/_schema.yaml =====
# docs/specs/_schema.yaml
# Documentation of the spec-manifest structure. Never executed.

schema_version: "1.0"

required_fields:
  - id          # e.g. iiot-001, dataops-002, webapp-001, cross-001 — MUST match the ID emitted by lib/*-audit.ts
  - profile     # iiot | dataops | webapp | cross
  - severity    # critical | error | warning | info
  - title
  - rationale   # WHY this matters, written from the domain's perspective (OT, data, or web)
  - checks      # list of checks (see check_types)
  - fix         # concrete terminal-level remediation
  - references  # list of {book | standard, concept} — MUST cite real sources only

optional_fields:
  - status
  - file_hint   # typical file where the pattern lives

check_types:
  - pattern_match   # fields: pattern, must_exist, file_glob, error_when, message
  - file_exists     # fields: path_glob, error_when, message
  - file_absent     # fields: path_glob, error_when, message

severity_weights:
  critical: 20
  error: 15
  warning: 10
  info: 2
===== END FILE: docs/specs/_schema.yaml =====

# docs/specs/dataops.yaml — DataOps & Analytics Engineering audit rules
#
# Synthesizes:
#   - Practical DataOps (Atwal)          → environment parity, testing, CI
#   - Fundamentals of Data Engineering   → lifecycle, security undercurrent, orchestration
#   - Designing Data-Intensive Apps      → schema evolution, reliability
#   - Analytics Engineering with SQL/dbt → modular modeling, ref/source
#   - Data Quality Fundamentals          → observability, quality gates, runbooks
#   - The Data Warehouse Toolkit         → dimensional modeling, surrogate keys, SCD
#   - Data Management at Scale           → data products, contracts, governance
version: "1.0"
profile: dataops

rules:
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # SECTION 1 — Security, Secrets & Infrastructure Governance
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  - id: dataops-sec-001
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: critical
    title: "Hardcoded secret in pipeline code"
    rationale: >
      A hardcoded password, token, or API key committed to source control stays
      exposed in Git history even after removal. Secrets belong in the runtime
      environment or a secret manager, never in the codebase. This violates the
      security undercurrent of the data engineering lifecycle and DataOps
      governance practices.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "(?i)(password|secret_key|api_key|access_token|private_key)\\s*[:=]\\s*[\"'][^\"']{8,}[\"']"
        must_exist: false
        file_glob: "**/*.{py,sql,yaml,yml,sh,json,env}"
        error_when: "pattern_present"
        message: "Hardcoded secret or credential detected. Use environment variables or a secret store."
    fix: |
      Move the secret to an environment variable or a secret manager.
      Add the real secret file to .gitignore and rotate the exposed credential.
    references:
      - book: "Practical DataOps"
        concept: "security governance"
      - book: "Fundamentals of Data Engineering"
        concept: "security undercurrent"

  - id: dataops-sec-002
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: critical
    title: "Database password embedded in connection string"
    rationale: >
      An unencrypted connection URI with an embedded password exposes credentials
      in code, logs, and version history. Connection secrets must be injected at
      runtime, not baked into pipeline configuration.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "(?i)(postgres|mysql|snowflake|redshift|bigquery|clickhouse)://[^:]+:[^@]+@"
        must_exist: false
        file_glob: "**/*.{py,yaml,yml,json,toml,ini}"
        error_when: "pattern_present"
        message: "Unencrypted connection string with embedded password detected."
    fix: |
      Replace the embedded password with an environment variable reference, e.g.
      postgres://user:${DB_PASSWORD}@host/db, and load DB_PASSWORD from a secret
      manager at runtime.
    references:
      - book: "Designing Data-Intensive Applications"
        concept: "reproducibility and secure configuration"

  - id: dataops-infra-001
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: warning
    title: "No Infrastructure-as-Code definition found"
    rationale: >
      Data platform infrastructure provisioned manually or through click-ops is
      not reproducible or auditable. The data architecture undercurrent of the
      data engineering lifecycle requires infrastructure to be declared as
      version-controlled code.
    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "{terraform/**/*.tf,cloudformation/**/*.yaml,pulumi/**/*.py,k8s/**/*.yaml}"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "No Infrastructure-as-Code declaration found. Provision data platform infrastructure as version-controlled code."
    fix: |
      Provision storage, compute, and IAM resources with Terraform, CloudFormation,
      Pulumi, or Kubernetes manifests, and commit the definitions to the repository.
    references:
      - book: "Fundamentals of Data Engineering"
        concept: "data architecture undercurrent"

  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  # SECTION 2 — Transformation & dbt Modularity
  # ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  - id: dataops-dbt-001
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: error
    title: "Hardcoded relation reference in dbt model"
    rationale: >
      Hardcoded database.schema.table paths break environment portability and
      prevent dbt from building the DAG required for correct execution order and
      lineage. Models must reference relations through ref() and source().
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "(?i)(from|join)\\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_]+\\.[a-zA-Z0-9_]+\\.[a-zA-Z0-9_]+"
        must_exist: false
        file_glob: "models/**/*.sql"
        error_when: "pattern_present"
        message: "Hardcoded three-part relation reference found in dbt model. Use ref() or source()."
    fix: |
      Replace hardcoded FROM/JOIN targets with dbt Jinja references:
      - internal dbt models: {{ ref('model_name') }}
      - raw source tables: {{ source('source_name', 'table_name') }}
    references:
      - book: "Analytics Engineering with SQL and dbt"
        concept: "explicit dbt lineage via ref and source"

  - id: dataops-dbt-002
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: error
    title: "Missing dbt_project.yml root manifest"
    rationale: >
      A repository containing dbt models without dbt_project.yml cannot be parsed
      or executed by the dbt CLI or CI tooling. The root manifest is the required
      entry point that identifies a directory as a dbt project.
    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "dbt_project.yml"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "dbt_project.yml is missing from the repository root."
    fix: |
      Initialize the dbt project with `dbt init` or ensure dbt_project.yml is
      committed at the repository root with name, version, and profile defined.
    references:
      - book: "Analytics Engineering with SQL and dbt"
        concept: "dbt project structure"

  - id: dataops-dbt-003
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: error
    title: "Missing models directory in dbt project"
    rationale: >
      A dbt project without a models directory has no transformation logic and
      cannot build a data pipeline. The models folder is the mandatory home for
      all SQL transformation models.
    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "models/"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "models/ directory is missing from the dbt repository."
    fix: |
      Create the models/ directory at the repository root and organize SQL
      transformations into staging/, intermediate/, and marts/ subfolders.
    references:
      - book: "Analytics Engineering with SQL and dbt"
        concept: "modular data modeling"

  - id: dataops-dbt-004
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: warning
    title: "Missing packages.yml for macros directory"
    rationale: >
      Using custom macros or third-party package macros without a packages.yml
      manifest causes compilation failures in CI/CD. Package dependencies must be
      declared explicitly for reproducible macro resolution.
    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "packages.yml"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "packages.yml manifest missing while a macros/ directory is present."
    fix: |
      Create a packages.yml file at the project root and declare all required dbt
      packages with explicit version pins, then run `dbt deps`.
    references:
      - book: "Analytics Engineering with SQL and dbt"
        concept: "dbt packages and macros"

    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: error
    title: "No automated test suite found"
    rationale: >
      Data software without unit or integration tests cannot be validated during

      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "{tests/**,test/**,pytest.ini}"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "Automated testing directory missing. Data software must include version-controlled test suites."
    fix: |
      Add a tests/ directory with pytest unit and integration tests, and wire them
      into the CI pipeline.
    references:
      - book: "Practical DataOps"
        concept: "automated testing and CI"

  - id: dataops-fde-001
    status: draft
    profile: dataops

    title: "Pipeline orchestration not defined as code"
    rationale: >
      Time-based cron scripts or manual pipeline execution violate the DataOps

    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "{dags/**/*.py,orchestration/**/*.py,workflows/**/*.yaml,dagster_app/**/*.py}"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "No pipeline orchestration code found. Declare workflows as code (DAGs)."
    fix: |
      Define data pipelines as code using Apache Airflow (dags/*.py), Dagster, or
      Prefect, and commit them to version control.
    references:

  - id: dataops-atwal-003
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: warning
    title: "Missing dependency lockfile"
    rationale: >
      Without a lockfile, dependency versions drift between environments, causing
      silent breakage and non-reproducible pipeline runs. Environment parity is a
      core DataOps requirement.
    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "{requirements.txt,Pipfile.lock,poetry.lock,environment.yml,conda-lock.yml}"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "Dependency lockfile missing. Pin package versions for reproducible pipeline execution."
    fix: |
      Generate a lockfile with `poetry lock`, `pip freeze > requirements.txt`, or
      `conda-lock -f environment.yml`, and commit it.
    references:
      - book: "Practical DataOps"
        concept: "environment parity"

    title: "No schema IDL for streaming events"

  # SECTION 5 — Dimensional Modeling & Domain Data Products

  - id: dataops-kimball-001
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: error
    title: "Fact model joins on natural key instead of surrogate key"
    rationale: >
      Joining fact models directly on natural operational codes couples the
      warehouse to source-system keys and breaks when those codes change. Kimball
      dimensional modeling requires surrogate keys for dimensional joins.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "(?i)join\\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_]+\\s+on\\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_.]+\\.(id|code|natural_key)\\s*="
        must_exist: false
        file_glob: "models/marts/facts/**/*.sql"
        error_when: "pattern_present"
        message: "Fact table model joins on a natural operational key. Use warehouse surrogate keys (_sk) for dimensional joins."
    fix: |
      Generate surrogate keys for dimensions and join facts to dimensions on the
      surrogate key column (e.g. customer_sk) instead of the natural key.
    references:
      - book: "The Data Warehouse Toolkit"
        concept: "surrogate keys"

  - id: dataops-kimball-002
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: error
    title: "SCD Type 2 dimension lacks effective-date tracking"
    rationale: >
      A slowly changing dimension that does not track effective dates cannot
      preserve history or answer point-in-time queries. SCD Type 2 models require
      valid-from/valid-to tracking columns.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "(?i)(effective_at|valid_from|dbt_valid_from)\\b"
        must_exist: true
        file_glob: "models/marts/dimensions/**/*.sql"
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "SCD Type 2 dimension model lacks required effective-date tracking columns."
    fix: |
      Add effective_at / valid_from / valid_to columns to the dimension model to
      track historical state, or use dbt snapshot to manage SCD Type 2.
    references:
      - book: "The Data Warehouse Toolkit"
        concept: "slowly changing dimensions"

  - id: dataops-kimball-003
    status: draft
    profile: dataops
    severity: warning
    title: "Missing enterprise bus matrix document"

    status: draft

    title: "No version-controlled data contracts found"
    rationale: >

    checks:

  - id: dataops-004
    profile: dataops
    status: draft
    severity: warning
    title: "No data lineage manifest found"
    rationale: >
      Pipelines without a committed lineage manifest (source → transform →

      incident. Lineage belongs in version control next to the pipeline code.
    checks:

        path_glob: "{lineage/**,docs/lineage*,**/lineage.{json,yaml,yml}}"

    profile: dataops
    status: draft
    severity: warning
    title: "No data catalog reference found"
    rationale: >
      Without a catalog reference, downstream consumers cannot discover the
      datasets their pipelines depend on, and governance has no authoritative
      inventory to enforce retention or access policy.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "datahub|amundsen|atlas|openmetadata|unity-catalog|glue-catalog"
        must_exist: true
        file_glob: "**/*.{py,yaml,yml,json,toml}"
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No data catalog integration detected"
    fix: |
      Register datasets with a data catalog (DataHub, Amundsen, OpenMetadata,
      Unity Catalog, or Glue Catalog) and reference the catalog ID in the
      dataset's YAML contract.
    references:
      - book: "Data Management at Scale"
        concept: "data-catalog"
      - book: "Practical DataOps"
        concept: "self-service-discovery"

  - id: dataops-006
    profile: dataops
    status: draft
    severity: error
    title: "PII columns not tagged in dataset schema"
    rationale: >
      Pipelines that touch personally identifiable information without explicit
      column-level tagging cannot enforce downstream masking, retention, or
      access controls. GDPR and CCPA compliance depend on this classification
      being explicit and version-controlled.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "(?i)(email|ssn|phone|dob|address|passport|national_id)"
        must_exist: true
        file_glob: "**/*.{sql,yml,yaml}"
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No PII column detection — verify schemas declare PII tags where applicable"
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "{pii/**,docs/pii*,**/pii-tags.{yml,yaml,json}}"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "No PII tag registry found — add pii-tags.yml with column classification"
    fix: |
      Create a pii-tags.yml registry classifying every column by sensitivity
      (public / internal / confidential / PII) and reference it from each
      dataset contract. Wire masking policies into the warehouse so tagged
      columns are automatically redacted for non-privileged roles.
    references:
      - book: "Data Management at Scale"
        concept: "data-governance"
      - book: "Designing Data-Intensive Applications"
        concept: "privacy-and-security"

===== FILE: docs/specs/iiot.yaml =====
version: "1.0"
profile: iiot
rules:
  - id: iiot-001
    status: draft
    profile: iiot
    severity: critical
    title: "Modbus exception handling missing"
    rationale: >
      Unhandled Modbus exceptions cause silent data loss and unplanned downtime
      in production PLC-connected systems. IEC 62443 requires explicit fault
      containment at every protocol boundary.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "ModbusIOException|ModbusException"
        file_glob: "**/*.py"
        must_exist: true
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No Modbus exception handling found in Python sources."
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "mock.*modbus|modbus.*mock|ModbusSimulatorServer"
        file_glob: "**/mock/**/*modbus*"
        must_exist: true
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No Modbus simulator/mock found for offline testing."
    fix: |
      Wrap every Modbus call in try/except ModbusIOException and provide a
      ModbusSimulatorServer fixture for unit tests so CI passes without hardware.

      Example:
        from pymodbus.exceptions import ModbusIOException
        try:
            result = client.read_holding_registers(0, 10)
        except ModbusIOException as exc:
            logger.error("Modbus read failed: %s", exc)
            raise
    references:
      - book: "Industrial Network Security"
        concept: "Chapter 4 – Protocol-level fault containment"
      - book: "PLC Controls with Structured Text"
        concept: "Chapter 7 – Exception handling in cyclic tasks"
      - standard: "IEC 62443-3-3 SR 3.5"
        concept: "fault containment at protocol boundaries"

  - id: iiot-002
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented
    severity: error
    title: "Hardcoded PLC IP address detected"
    rationale: >
      Hardcoded private IP addresses couple code to a single physical network
      segment and expose internal topology in version control. IEC 62443
      requires network-segment isolation and configuration externalisation.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "192\\.168\\.|10\\.0\\.|172\\.(1[6-9]|2[0-9]|3[01])\\."
        file_glob: "**/*.py"
        must_exist: false
        error_when: "pattern_present"
        message: "Hardcoded private IP found – move PLC_HOST to environment variable."
    fix: |
      Replace the literal IP with an environment variable:
        import os
        PLC_HOST = os.environ["PLC_HOST"]  # e.g. 192.168.1.10
      Store the real value in .env (git-ignored) and document it in .env.example.
    references:
      - book: "Practical Industrial Data Networks"
        concept: "Chapter 2 – Configuration management for field devices"
      - standard: "IEC 62443-2-1 SP.03.02"
        concept: "configuration externalisation"

  - id: iiot-003
    status: draft
    profile: iiot
    severity: warning
    title: "Python dependencies not pinned"
    rationale: >
      Unpinned dependencies allow silent upgrades that can break fieldbus
      driver behaviour between deployments.
    checks:
      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "requirements*.txt"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "No requirements*.txt found."
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "==\\d"
        file_glob: "requirements*.txt"
        must_exist: true
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "Dependencies appear unpinned – use == specifiers."
    fix: |
      Pin every runtime dependency:
        pip freeze > requirements.txt
      or manually:
        pymodbus==3.6.3
        opcua==0.98.13
    references:
      - book: "Automating Manufacturing Systems with PLCs"
        concept: "Appendix B – Reproducible Python environments"

  - id: iiot-004
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented
    severity: critical
    title: "LOTO / safety-procedure documentation missing"
    rationale: >
      Repositories controlling physical machinery must ship lockout–tagout
      documentation alongside the code so maintenance teams can safely
      de-energise equipment before any software change is applied.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "loto|lockout|LOTO|LOCKOUT"
        file_glob: "{docs/safety/**,LOCKOUT*.md,SAFETY.md,docs/loto/**}"
        must_exist: true
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No LOTO / lockout-tagout documentation found."
    fix: |
      Create docs/safety/loto.md referencing your site-level LOTO procedure
      and link it from README.md under a Safety section.
    references:
      - book: "Industrial Network Security"
        concept: "Chapter 9 – Physical security and maintenance windows"
      - standard: "NFPA 70E Article 120"
        concept: "lockout-tagout procedure"

  - id: iiot-005
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented
    severity: error
    title: "Network topology / zone diagram missing"

      so that security assessments and incident response can correctly scope

    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "topology|network"
        file_glob: "{docs/network/**,docs/architecture/network*,**/*topology*.yaml,**/*topology*.yml,**/*topology*.md,**/*topology*.d2}"
        must_exist: true

    fix: |

  - id: iiot-006
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented
    severity: warning

        path_glob: "{plc,scada}/**/*.{st,scl,lad,fbd,sfc}"

      - type: file_exists
        path_glob: "{CHANGELOG*,docs/CHANGELOG*}"
        error_when: "file_absent"
        message: "No CHANGELOG found for PLC/SCADA change tracking."
    fix: |
      1. Store structured text / ladder sources under plc/ or scada/.
      2. Add a CHANGELOG.md entry for every release, referencing the ticket ID.
    references:

  - id: iiot-007
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented

    rationale: >
      Hardcoded COM ports and :502 addresses prevent multi-environment

  - id: iiot-008
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented
    severity: warning

    rationale: >
      Tests that require live hardware are not runnable in CI. A mock or
      simulator layer decouples logic tests from physical device availability.
    checks:

        pattern: "modbus|Modbus"
        file_glob: "{tests/**/*modbus*,__mocks__/**,conftest.py,mock_server.py}"
        must_exist: true

      mock that returns realistic register maps for your device profile.
    references:

        concept: "Chapter 12 – Software-in-the-loop testing"

  - id: iiot-009
    profile: iiot
    status: implemented
    severity: warning
    title: "Protocol client timeout not set"
    rationale: >
      Without explicit timeouts, a stalled PLC or network partition causes
      the application to block indefinitely, preventing watchdog recovery.
    checks:
      - type: pattern_match
        pattern: "timeout\\s*[=:]"
        file_glob: "**/*.py"
        must_exist: true
        error_when: "pattern_absent"
        message: "No explicit timeout found in Python sources."
    fix: |
      Pass a timeout to every protocol client:
        ModbusTcpClient(host, timeout=3)
        asyncio.wait_for(coro, timeout=5.0)
    references:
      - book: "Industrial Network Security"
        concept: "Chapter 5 – Resilience and watchdog patterns"

===== FILE: lib/types.ts =====
// lib/types.ts
// ============================================================================
// Shared domain types for RepoScope.
//
// Every other module should import from here. Avoid duplicate shapes.
//
// Important invariant:
//   Every TypeScript-emitted finding.ruleId MUST match a YAML rule id in
//   docs/specs/*.yaml, public/knowledge/specs.json, and
//   public/knowledge/graph.json.
// ============================================================================

// ─── GitHub tree item ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export interface GitHubTreeItem {
  path: string;
  mode?: string;
  type: "blob" | "tree";
  sha?: string;
  size?: number;
  url?: string;
}

// ─── Severity ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export type Severity = "critical" | "error" | "warning" | "info";

/**
 * Backward-compatible alias used by UI components (Board.tsx, ScoreBoard.tsx).
 * Prefer `Severity` in new code.
 */
export type FindingSeverity = Severity;

export const SEVERITY_WEIGHTS: Record<Severity, number> = {
  critical: 20,
  error: 15,
  warning: 10,
  info: 2,
};

// ─── Canonical audit profiles ───────────────────────────────────────────────
export type CanonicalAuditProfile =
  | "dataops"
  | "iiot"
  | "webapp"
  | "cross"
  | "graph";

export type AuditProfile = CanonicalAuditProfile | "web";

export interface AuditProfileConfig {
  id: CanonicalAuditProfile;
  label: string;
  description: string;
}

export const CANONICAL_AUDIT_PROFILES: readonly CanonicalAuditProfile[] = [
  "dataops",
  "iiot",
  "webapp",
  "cross",
  "graph",
];

export const AUDIT_PROFILES: AuditProfileConfig[] = [
  {
    id: "dataops",
    label: "DataOps & Analytics",
    description:
      "Checks notebooks, dbt models, lockfiles, schema validation, and raw data leakage.",
  },
  {
    id: "iiot",
    label: "Industrial / PLC",
    description:
      "Validates deterministic code, Modbus mocks, SCADA tests, and OT safety patterns.",
  },
  {
    id: "webapp",
    label: "General Web App",
    description:
      "Standard CI/CD, README, dependency, security, and deployment audit.",
  },
  {
    id: "cross",
    label: "Cross-Domain",
    description:
      "Composite rules that span IIoT, DataOps, web, and knowledge-graph concerns.",
  },
  {
    id: "graph",
    label: "Graph RAG / Knowledge Graph",
    description:
      "Checks graph grounding, citation integrity, schema stability, and GraphRAG audit rules.",
  },
];

export const PROFILE_ALIASES: Record<string, CanonicalAuditProfile> = {
  web: "webapp",
  website: "webapp",
  general: "webapp",
  graphrag: "graph",
  "graph-rag": "graph",
};

function isCanonicalAuditProfile(value: string): value is CanonicalAuditProfile {
  return (CANONICAL_AUDIT_PROFILES as readonly string[]).includes(value);
}

export function normalizeAuditProfile(
  value: unknown
): CanonicalAuditProfile | null {
  if (typeof value !== "string") return null;
  const raw = value.trim().toLowerCase();
  if (!raw) return null;
  if (isCanonicalAuditProfile(raw)) return raw;
  return PROFILE_ALIASES[raw] ?? null;
}

export function canonicalAuditProfile(
  value: unknown,
  fallback: CanonicalAuditProfile = "webapp"
): CanonicalAuditProfile {
  return normalizeAuditProfile(value) ?? fallback;
}

// ─── Rule ID convention ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
export const RULE_ID_REGEX =
  /^(iiot|dataops|webapp|cross|general|graph)(-[a-z0-9]+)*-\d{3}[a-z]?$/;

export function isValidRuleId(value: unknown): boolean {
  return typeof value === "string" && RULE_ID_REGEX.test(value);
}

// ─── Finding ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
//
// ruleId MUST match the YAML spec id.
// The `detail` field is canonical here. Do NOT add a `message` field to
// Finding — use toInsightFinding() to convert for the AI layer.
export interface Finding {
  ruleId: string;
  severity: Severity;
  title: string;
  detail: string;
  fix: string;
  weight: number;
  profile?: AuditProfile;
  file?: string;
}

export interface ScoredFinding extends Finding {
  points: number;
}

export interface AuditScore {
  score: number;
  critical: number;
  errors: number;
  warnings: number;
  infos: number;
  findings: Finding[];
  items: ScoredFinding[];
  counts: {
    critical: number;
    error: number;
    warning: number;
    info: number;
  };
  summary: string;
}

// ─── Knowledge graph / citation gate types ──────────────────────────────────
export interface KnowledgeNode {
  id: string;
  concept: string;
  source: string;
  chapter?: string;
  page?: number;
  pageRange?: string;
  ruleId?: string;
  profile?: CanonicalAuditProfile;
  verificationStatus?:
    | "source-backed"
    | "synthesis"
    | "not-established"
    | "needs-verification";
}

export interface CitationResult {
  ruleId: string;
  grounded: boolean;
  node?: KnowledgeNode;
}

export interface GroundedSources {
  rules: string[];
  concepts: string[];
  books: string[];
}

export interface SmartDiagnosis {
  summary: string;
  citations: CitationResult[];
  strippedCount: number;
}

// ─── Insight finding shape used by the AI layer ─────────────────────────────
//
// INTENTIONALLY LOOSE:
//   - profile and severity are `string` here (not the strict unions) so that
//     a caller can pass legacy values like "web" or "info" without TS fighting
//     them.
//   - Both `message` and `detail` exist; use toInsightFinding() to fill both
//     so downstream prompt builders never see an empty body.
export interface InsightFinding {
  id?: string;
  ruleId?: string;
  profile?: string;
  severity?: string;
  title?: string;
  message?: string;
  detail?: string;
  fix?: string;
  file?: string;
}

/**
 * Normalize any Finding-like object into the canonical InsightFinding shape.
 *
 * This is the SINGLE source of truth for the message/detail alias. Call this
 * at every boundary where a raw audit finding enters the AI layer.
 *
 *   Finding.detail     -> InsightFinding.message AND .detail
 *   InsightFinding.id  -> InsightFinding.ruleId (backfilled)
 *   InsightFinding.ruleId -> InsightFinding.id (backfilled)
 */
export function toInsightFinding(
  finding: Finding | InsightFinding
): InsightFinding {
  const id =
    (finding as Finding).ruleId ??
    (finding as InsightFinding).id ??
    (finding as InsightFinding).ruleId;

  const detail =
    (finding as Finding).detail ??
    (finding as InsightFinding).message ??
    (finding as InsightFinding).detail ??
    "";

  const severity =
    typeof finding.severity === "string" ? finding.severity : undefined;

  const profile =
    typeof finding.profile === "string" ? finding.profile : undefined;

  return {
    id,
    ruleId: id,
    profile,
    severity,
    title: finding.title,
    message: detail,
    detail,
    fix: finding.fix,
    file: finding.file,
  };
}

  // ── Industrial ML (Layer M: global lens — ALWAYS applied) ──────

  "industrial machine learning",
  "outlier analysis",
];

export const KNOWN_SOURCES: readonly string[] = [...KNOWN_BOOKS, ...KNOWN_STANDARDS];

export function listKnownSources(): readonly string[] {
  return KNOWN_SOURCES;
}

/**
 * Normalize a source/book title for robust comparison.
 *
 * Handles:
 *   - Unicode curly quotes (’, “, ”, –, —)
 *   - Collapsing whitespace
 *   - Lowercasing
 *
 * Use this in citation-gate.ts before comparing against KNOWN_SOURCES.
 */
export function normalizeSourceName(value: string): string {
  return value
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[\u2018\u2019\u02BC]/g, "'")
    .replace(/[\u201C\u201D]/g, '"')
    .replace(/[\u2013\u2014]/g, "-")
    .replace(/\s+/g, " ")
    .trim();
}

===== FILE: lib/knowledge/graph.ts =====
// lib/knowledge/graph.ts — SERVER ONLY. Loads the static knowledge graph
// produced by scripts/kg/assemble_graph.py (Day 15) and provides traversal.
// Graceful degradation: if graph.json is missing, grounding silently
// disables and the audit engine keeps working (resilience by design).
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";

export interface GraphNode {
  id: string;
  type: "rule" | "concept" | "book";
  label: string;
  severity?: string;
  profile?: string;
  rationale?: string;
  fix?: string;
  definition?: string;
  domain?: string;
  kind?: string; // for book nodes: "book" | "standard"
  source?: { book?: string; chapter?: string; page_range?: string };
}

export interface GraphEdge {
  source: string;
  target: string;
  type: string;
  weight?: number;
}

export interface KnowledgeGraph {
  version: string;
  generated_at?: string;
  stats?: Record<string, number>;
  nodes: GraphNode[];
  edges: GraphEdge[];
}

export interface ConceptRef {
  label: string;
  definition: string;
  book?: string;
}

export interface OneHop {
  rule: GraphNode | null;
  concepts: ConceptRef[];
  books: string[];
}

let cached: KnowledgeGraph | null | undefined; // undefined = not yet loaded

export function loadGraph(): KnowledgeGraph | null {
  if (cached !== undefined) return cached;
  try {
    const file = path.join(process.cwd(), "public", "knowledge", "graph.json");
    if (!fs.existsSync(file)) {
      cached = null;
      return cached;
    }
    cached = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, "utf-8")) as KnowledgeGraph;
  } catch (err) {
    console.warn("[graph] failed to load graph.json — grounding disabled:", err);
    cached = null;
  }
  return cached;
}

export function graphIsAvailable(): boolean {
  return loadGraph() !== null;
}

// ---- lazy indexes ----
const nodeById = new Map<string, GraphNode>();
const edgesFrom = new Map<string, GraphEdge[]>();
let indexed = false;

function ensureIndex(): void {
  if (indexed) return;
  const g = loadGraph();
  if (g) {
    for (const n of g.nodes) nodeById.set(n.id, n);
    for (const e of g.edges) {
      const arr = edgesFrom.get(e.source) ?? [];
      arr.push(e);
      edgesFrom.set(e.source, arr);
    }
  }
  indexed = true;
}

export function hasRule(id: string): boolean {
  ensureIndex();
  return nodeById.has(`rule:${id.toLowerCase()}`);
}

export function getRule(id: string): GraphNode | null {
  ensureIndex();
  return nodeById.get(`rule:${id.toLowerCase()}`) ?? null;
}

export function loadValidRuleIds(): Set<string> {
  ensureIndex();
  const ids = new Set<string>();
  for (const [id, node] of nodeById) {
    if (node.type === "rule") ids.add(id.replace(/^rule:/, ""));
  }
  return ids;
}

/** One-hop BFS from a rule node (per Graph Algorithms, ch. 2 — bounded traversal). */
export function expandOneHop(ruleId: string): OneHop {
  ensureIndex();
  const rid = `rule:${ruleId.toLowerCase()}`;
  const rule = nodeById.get(rid) ?? null;
  if (!rule) return { rule: null, concepts: [], books: [] };

  const concepts: ConceptRef[] = [];
  const books: string[] = [];

  for (const edge of edgesFrom.get(rid) ?? []) {
    const target = nodeById.get(edge.target);
    if (!target) continue;
    if (target.type === "concept") {
      concepts.push({
        label: target.label,
        definition: target.definition ?? "",
        book: target.source?.book,
      });
    } else if (target.type === "book") {
      books.push(target.label);
    }
  }
  return { rule, concepts, books };
}

export function subgraphForProfile(profile: string): { nodes: GraphNode[]; edges: GraphEdge[] } {
  const g = loadGraph();
  if (!g) return { nodes: [], edges: [] };
  const nodes = g.nodes.filter(
    (n) =>
      n.profile === profile ||
      n.domain === profile ||
      (n.type === "rule" && n.id.startsWith(`rule:${profile}`))
  );
  const ids = new Set(nodes.map((n) => n.id));
  const edges = g.edges.filter((e) => ids.has(e.source) && ids.has(e.target));
  return { nodes, edges };
}

export function graphStats(): Record<string, number> {
  return loadGraph()?.stats ?? {};
}
export function detectCrossDomain(
  findings: ReadonlyArray<{ profile?: string }>
): { crossDomain: boolean; domains: string[] } {
  const domains = Array.from(
    new Set(
      findings
        .map((f) => f.profile)
        .filter((p): p is string => typeof p === "string" && p.length > 0)
    )
  );

  return {
    crossDomain: domains.length > 1,
    domains,
  };
}
===== END FILE: lib/knowledge/graph.ts =====

===== FILE: lib/knowledge/citation-gate.ts =====
// lib/knowledge/citation-gate.ts
// Day 18 - machine-verifiable sources.
// Strips every LLM claim of "page N", "section X.Y", or "according to <Name>"
// UNLESS the sentence also cites a known book/standard from the registry.
// Validates cited rule IDs against the knowledge graph.
// This is the RepoScope implementation of the "never fabricate references" rule.

import { listKnownSources } from "./books";
import { graphIsAvailable, loadValidRuleIds } from "./graph";

export interface CitationResult {
  text: string;
  grounded: boolean;
  validCitations: string[];
  strippedCitations: string[];
  stripped: number;
}

export interface GateOptions {
  validRuleIds?: Set<string>;        // inject in tests; defaults to graph
  knownSources?: readonly string[];  // inject in tests; defaults to registry
}

// Rule-ID convention: iiot-001, dataops-003a, iiot-modbus-001, cross-001 ...
const RULE_ID_RE =
  /\b(?:iiot|dataops|webapp|cross|general)(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*-\d{3}[a-z]?\b/gi;

const SENTENCE_RE = /[^.!?\n]+[.!?]/g;

// Suspicious-source patterns: `test` for detection, `replace` for stripping.
// Trigger phrases are case-insensitive on the first letter only; the name
// after them stays case-sensitive so common English words like "the" or
// "this" after "per" do not trigger a false positive.
const SUSPICIOUS_PATTERNS: Array<{ test: RegExp; replace: RegExp }> = [
  { test: /\b(?:page|pp?\.)\s*\d+/i,
    replace: /\b(?:page|pp?\.)\s*\d+(?:\s*[-\u2013]\s*\d+)?/gi },
  { test: /\b(?:section|clause|chapter)\s+\d+(?:\.\d+)?/i,
    replace: /\b(?:section|clause|chapter)\s+\d+(?:\.\d+)?/gi },
  { test: /\b(?:[Aa]ccording to|[Aa]s stated by|[Pp]er)\s+[A-Z][a-z]{2,}/,
    replace: /\b(?:[Aa]ccording to|[Aa]s stated by|[Pp]er)\s+[A-Z][a-z]{2,}/g },
];

export function validateCitations(rawText: string, options: GateOptions = {}): CitationResult {
  const validRuleIds =
    options.validRuleIds ?? (graphIsAvailable() ? loadValidRuleIds() : new Set<string>());
  const knownSources = options.knownSources ?? listKnownSources();

  // ---- 1. Rule-ID citations ----
  const citedRuleIds = Array.from(
    new Set((rawText.match(RULE_ID_RE) ?? []).map((m) => m.toLowerCase()))
  );
  const validRules = citedRuleIds.filter((id) => validRuleIds.has(id));
  const invalidRules = citedRuleIds.filter((id) => !validRuleIds.has(id));

  // ---- 2. Known book / standard citations ----
  const lower = rawText.toLowerCase();
  const citedSources = knownSources.filter((s) => lower.includes(s));

  // ---- 3. Sentence-level suspicious references ----
  let stripped = 0;
  const strippedCitations: string[] = [...invalidRules];
  let text = rawText;

  const sentences = rawText.match(SENTENCE_RE) ?? [rawText];
  for (const sentence of sentences) {
    const suspicious = SUSPICIOUS_PATTERNS.some((p) => p.test.test(sentence));
    if (!suspicious) continue;

    const sLower = sentence.toLowerCase();
    const hasKnownSource = knownSources.some((s) => sLower.includes(s));
    if (hasKnownSource) continue; // sourced sentence keeps its page/section refs

    let cleaned = sentence;
    for (const p of SUSPICIOUS_PATTERNS) {
      cleaned = cleaned.replace(p.replace, "[source unverified]");
    }
    if (cleaned !== sentence) {
      text = text.replace(sentence, cleaned); // literal first-occurrence replace
      stripped += 1;
      strippedCitations.push(sentence.trim().slice(0, 80));
    }
  }

  const allValid = [...new Set([...validRules, ...citedSources])];

  return {
    text,
    grounded: allValid.length > 0,
    validCitations: allValid,
    strippedCitations,
    stripped,
  };
}

===== FILE: lib/llm/adapters.ts =====
// lib/llm/adapters.ts
// Wire-format adapters. Every known LLM API falls into one of three shapes.
// Pure functions — fully testable offline. ESLint-9-safe (no `any`).
import type { ProviderConfig } from "./providers";

export interface LLMRequest {
  system: string;
  prompt: string;
  model: string;
  temperature?: number;
  maxTokens?: number;
}

export interface OutboundRequest {
  url: string;
  headers: Record<string, string>;
  body: Record<string, unknown>;
}

export interface ParsedResponse {
  content: string;
}

// ---- unknown-narrowing helpers (lint-safe) ----
function isRecord(v: unknown): v is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof v === "object" && v !== null;
}
function str(v: unknown): string | undefined {
  return typeof v === "string" ? v : undefined;
}
function partsText(v: unknown): string {
  if (!Array.isArray(v)) return "";
  return v
    .map((p) => (isRecord(p) ? str(p.text) ?? "" : ""))
    .join("")
    .trim();
}

export function buildRequest(
  config: ProviderConfig, req: LLMRequest, apiKey: string
): OutboundRequest {
  switch (config.apiFormat) {
    case "openai-compatible": return buildOpenAI(config, req, apiKey);
    case "anthropic":         return buildAnthropic(config, req, apiKey);
    case "google":            return buildGoogle(config, req, apiKey);
  }
}

export function parseResponse(config: ProviderConfig, data: unknown): ParsedResponse {
  switch (config.apiFormat) {
    case "openai-compatible": return parseOpenAI(data);
    case "anthropic":         return parseAnthropic(data);
    case "google":            return parseGoogle(data);
  }
}

// ---- 1. OpenAI-compatible: OpenAI, Groq, Mistral, DeepSeek, Together,
//         OpenRouter, Fireworks, Perplexity, xAI, Ollama, vLLM, LiteLLM ----
function buildOpenAI(c: ProviderConfig, r: LLMRequest, key: string): OutboundRequest {
  return {
    url: `${c.baseUrl}/chat/completions`,
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json",
      ...(key ? { Authorization: `Bearer ${key}` } : {}),
      ...c.extraHeaders,
    },
    body: {
      model: r.model,
      temperature: r.temperature ?? 0.1,
      max_tokens: r.maxTokens ?? 350,
      messages: [
        { role: "system", content: r.system },
        { role: "user", content: r.prompt },
      ],
    },
  };
}
function parseOpenAI(d: unknown): ParsedResponse {
  if (!isRecord(d) || !Array.isArray(d.choices) || d.choices.length === 0) return { content: "" };
  const first = d.choices[0];
  const content =
    isRecord(first) && isRecord(first.message) ? str(first.message.content)?.trim() ?? "" : "";
  return { content };
}

// ---- 2. Anthropic Messages API ----
function buildAnthropic(c: ProviderConfig, r: LLMRequest, key: string): OutboundRequest {
  return {
    url: `${c.baseUrl}/messages`,
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json",
      "x-api-key": key,
      "anthropic-version": "2023-06-01",
    },
    body: {
      model: r.model,
      max_tokens: r.maxTokens ?? 350,
      temperature: r.temperature ?? 0.1,
      system: r.system,
      messages: [{ role: "user", content: r.prompt }],
    },
  };
}
function parseAnthropic(d: unknown): ParsedResponse {
  const content = isRecord(d) ? partsText(d.content) : "";
  return { content };
}

// ---- 3. Google Gemini generateContent ----
function buildGoogle(c: ProviderConfig, r: LLMRequest, key: string): OutboundRequest {
  return {
    url: `${c.baseUrl}/models/${r.model}:generateContent`,
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", "x-goog-api-key": key },
    body: {
      systemInstruction: { parts: [{ text: r.system }] },
      contents: [{ role: "user", parts: [{ text: r.prompt }] }],
      generationConfig: {
        temperature: r.temperature ?? 0.1,
        maxOutputTokens: r.maxTokens ?? 350,
      },
    },
  };
}
function parseGoogle(d: unknown): ParsedResponse {
  if (!isRecord(d) || !Array.isArray(d.candidates) || d.candidates.length === 0) {
    return { content: "" };
  }
  const first = d.candidates[0];
  const content = isRecord(first) && isRecord(first.content) ? partsText(first.content.parts) : "";
  return { content };
}
===== END FILE: lib/llm/adapters.ts =====

===== FILE: lib/llm/client.ts =====
// lib/llm/client.ts — SERVER ONLY. Never import from a client component.
import type { ProviderConfig } from "./providers";
import { buildRequest, parseResponse, type LLMRequest } from "./adapters";

const TIMEOUT_MS = 9_000; // stays under Vercel Hobby's 10s function cap

export class LLMError extends Error {
  constructor(
    message: string,
    public status: number,
    public providerId: string
  ) {
    super(message);
    this.name = "LLMError";
  }
}

export interface LLMResponse {
  content: string;
  provider: string;
  model: string;
}

export async function generateText(
  config: ProviderConfig,
  req: LLMRequest,
  apiKey: string
): Promise<LLMResponse> {
  const { url, headers, body } = buildRequest(config, req, apiKey);

  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), TIMEOUT_MS);

  let res: Response;
  try {
    res = await fetch(url, {
      method: "POST",
      headers,
      body: JSON.stringify(body),
      signal: controller.signal,
    });
  } catch (err: unknown) {
    const name = err instanceof Error ? err.name : "";
    if (name === "AbortError") {
      throw new LLMError(`${config.id} timed out after ${TIMEOUT_MS / 1000}s`, 504, config.id);
    }
    const msg = err instanceof Error ? err.message : "network error";
    throw new LLMError(`Could not reach ${config.id}: ${msg}`, 502, config.id);
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timer);
  }

  if (!res.ok) {
    const raw = await res.text().catch(() => "");
    let detail = raw.slice(0, 200);
    try {
      const j = JSON.parse(raw) as Record<string, unknown>;
      const errObj = isRecord(j.error) ? j.error : null;
      detail = (typeof j.message === "string" && j.message) ||
               (errObj && typeof errObj.message === "string" && errObj.message) ||
               detail;
    } catch { /* keep raw */ }

    if (res.status === 401 || res.status === 403)
      throw new LLMError(`Invalid API key for ${config.id}. ${detail}`, 401, config.id);
    if (res.status === 429)
      throw new LLMError(`${config.id} rate limit exceeded. ${detail}`, 429, config.id);
    if (res.status === 404)
      throw new LLMError(`Model "${req.model}" not found on ${config.id}. ${detail}`, 404, config.id);
    throw new LLMError(`${config.id} error ${res.status}. ${detail}`, 502, config.id);
  }

  const data: unknown = await res.json().catch(() => null);
  const { content } = parseResponse(config, data);
  if (!content) throw new LLMError(`${config.id} returned an empty response.`, 502, config.id);

  return { content, provider: config.id, model: req.model };
}

function isRecord(v: unknown): v is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof v === "object" && v !== null;
}
===== END FILE: lib/llm/client.ts =====

===== FILE: app/api/insight/route.ts =====
// app/api/insight/route.ts — FINAL unified route.
//   Day 7:  providers, key resolution, rate limit
//   Day 17: grounded prompt (graph context)
//   Day 18: citation gate on output
//   Day 19: cross-domain mode emerges from multi-profile findings
//   Day 21: freshness metadata in the response
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from "next/server";
import { PROVIDERS, getProvider } from "@/lib/llm/providers";
import { generateText, LLMError } from "@/lib/llm/client";
import {
  buildInsightPrompt,
  buildGroundedPrompt,
  buildDiagnosisPrompt,
  type InsightFinding,
} from "@/lib/insight";
import { checkAIRateLimit } from "@/lib/rate-limiter";
import { validateCitations } from "@/lib/knowledge/citation-gate";
import { graphIsAvailable } from "@/lib/knowledge/graph";

export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"; // never cache

function getClientIp(req: NextRequest): string {
  return (
    req.headers.get("x-forwarded-for")?.split(",")[0]?.trim() ??
    req.headers.get("x-real-ip") ??
    "unknown"
  );
}

function listServerProviders(): string[] {
  return Object.values(PROVIDERS)
    .filter((p) => p.envVar && Boolean(process.env[p.envVar]))
    .map((p) => p.id);
}

/** GET — which providers are provisioned + whether the knowledge graph is live. */
export async function GET() {
  return NextResponse.json({
    serverProviders: listServerProviders(),
    defaultProvider: process.env.DEFAULT_AI_PROVIDER ?? "groq",
    dailyLimit: Number(process.env.AI_DAILY_LIMIT ?? 5),
    graph: graphIsAvailable(),
  });
}

export async function POST(request: NextRequest) {
  try {
    const body = await request.json();
    const {
      findings,
      profile,
      provider: providerId,
      model,
      mode,               // "insight" (default) | "diagnose"
      patterns,           // diagnose mode: detected error patterns
      logExcerpt,         // diagnose mode: truncated log text
      domain,             // diagnose mode: "data" | "ot" | "web" | "general"
    } = body as {
      findings?: InsightFinding[];
      profile?: string;
      provider?: string;
      model?: string;
      mode?: "insight" | "diagnose";
      patterns?: string[];
      logExcerpt?: string;
      domain?: "data" | "ot" | "web" | "general";
    };

    if (mode === "diagnose") {
      if (!Array.isArray(patterns) || typeof logExcerpt !== "string") {
        return NextResponse.json(
          { error: "diagnose mode requires patterns[] and logExcerpt" },
          { status: 400 }
        );
      }
    } else if (!Array.isArray(findings)) {
      return NextResponse.json({ error: "findings array is required" }, { status: 400 });
    }

    const provider = getProvider(providerId ?? process.env.DEFAULT_AI_PROVIDER ?? "groq");
    if (!provider) {
      return NextResponse.json(
        { error: `Unknown provider "${providerId}".`, available: Object.keys(PROVIDERS) },
        { status: 400 }
      );
    }

    let baseUrl = provider.baseUrl;
    let usedModel = model?.trim() || provider.defaultModel;

    if (provider.id === "custom") {
      const customBase = process.env.CUSTOM_LLM_BASE_URL;
      if (!customBase) {
        return NextResponse.json(
          { error: 'Provider "custom" requires CUSTOM_LLM_BASE_URL (and optionally CUSTOM_LLM_MODEL).' },
          { status: 503 }
        );
      }
      baseUrl = customBase.replace(/\/$/, "");
      usedModel = model?.trim() || process.env.CUSTOM_LLM_MODEL || "default";
    }

    // ---- Key resolution: BYOK header > server env > 503 (if key required) ----
    const userKey = request.headers.get("x-ai-key")?.trim() ?? "";
    const serverKey = provider.envVar ? process.env[provider.envVar] ?? "" : "";

    if (!userKey && !serverKey && provider.requiresKey) {
      return NextResponse.json(
        {
          error: `No API key available for provider "${provider.id}".`,
          serverProviders: listServerProviders(),
          hint: 'Send your own key via the "x-ai-key" header (BYOK), or pick a provisioned provider.',
        },
        { status: 503 }
      );
    }

    // Rate limit ONLY server-key usage — BYOK users pay for their own tokens.
    if (!userKey && serverKey) {
      const { allowed } = checkAIRateLimit(getClientIp(request));
      if (!allowed) {
        return NextResponse.json(
          {
            error: "Daily AI analysis limit reached.",
            hint: "Provide your own API key via the x-ai-key header to bypass this limit.",
          },
          { status: 429 }
        );
      }
    }

    // ---- Prompt selection ----
    let system: string;
    let prompt: string;
    let maxTokens: number;

    if (mode === "diagnose") {
      const built = buildDiagnosisPrompt({
        patterns: patterns ?? [],
        logExcerpt: logExcerpt ?? "",
        domain: domain ?? "general",
      });
      system = built.system;
      prompt = built.prompt;
      maxTokens = 400;
    } else if (graphIsAvailable() && (findings ?? []).some((f) => Boolean(f.id))) {
      // Day 17: grounded path — the default when the graph exists
      const built = buildGroundedPrompt(findings ?? [], profile);
      system = built.system;
      prompt = built.prompt;
      maxTokens = 300; // grounded answers stay tight (Vercel Hobby timeout headroom)
    } else {
      const built = buildInsightPrompt(findings ?? [], profile ?? "general");
      system = built.system;
      prompt = built.prompt;
      maxTokens = 350;
    }

    const response = await generateText(
      { ...provider, baseUrl },
      { system, prompt, model: usedModel, temperature: 0.1, maxTokens },
      userKey || serverKey
    );

    // ---- Day 18: Citation Gate ----
    const gate = validateCitations(response.content);

    return NextResponse.json(
      {
        insight: gate.text,
        grounded: gate.grounded,
        citations: gate.validCitations,
        stripped: gate.stripped,
        provider: response.provider,
        model: response.model,
        generatedAt: new Date().toISOString(), // Day 21: freshness metadata
        usingByok: Boolean(userKey),
        mode: mode ?? "insight",
      },
      { headers: { "Cache-Control": "no-store" } }
    );
  } catch (error: unknown) {
    if (error instanceof LLMError) {
      console.error(`[insight:${error.providerId}]`, error.message); // message only — never the key
      const status = error.status === 401 || error.status === 429 ? error.status : 502;
      return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status });
    }
    console.error("Insight route error:", error);
    return NextResponse.json({ error: "AI analysis failed" }, { status: 500 });
  }
}
===== END FILE: app/api/insight/route.ts =====

===== FILE: __tests__/llm-layer.test.ts =====
// __tests__/llm-layer.test.ts — fully offline; runs inside existing CI gates.
import { buildRequest, parseResponse } from "@/lib/llm/adapters";
import { PROVIDERS, getProvider } from "@/lib/llm/providers";
import { buildInsightPrompt, buildGroundedPrompt } from "@/lib/insight";
import { validateCitations } from "@/lib/knowledge/citation-gate";
import { KNOWN_SOURCES } from "@/lib/knowledge/books";

const req = { system: "s", prompt: "p", model: "m" };

describe("adapters (Day 6)", () => {
  it("builds OpenAI-compatible wire format", () => {
    const r = buildRequest(PROVIDERS.groq, req, "key1");
    expect(r.url).toBe("https://api.groq.com/openai/v1/chat/completions");
    expect(r.headers.Authorization).toBe("Bearer key1");
    expect(r.body.messages).toEqual([
      { role: "system", content: "s" },
      { role: "user", content: "p" },
    ]);
  });

  it("builds Anthropic wire format with version header", () => {
    const r = buildRequest(PROVIDERS.anthropic, req, "key2");
    expect(r.headers["x-api-key"]).toBe("key2");
    expect(r.headers["anthropic-version"]).toBe("2023-06-01");
    expect(r.body.system).toBe("s");
  });

  it("builds Google wire format with model in URL", () => {
    const r = buildRequest(PROVIDERS.google, req, "key3");
    expect(r.url).toContain("/models/m:generateContent");
    expect(r.headers["x-goog-api-key"]).toBe("key3");
  });

  it("omits Authorization for keyless providers (Ollama)", () => {
    const r = buildRequest(PROVIDERS.ollama, req, "");
    expect(r.headers.Authorization).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it("parses all three response shapes", () => {
    expect(parseResponse(PROVIDERS.groq, { choices: [{ message: { content: " hi " } }] }).content).toBe("hi");
    expect(parseResponse(PROVIDERS.anthropic, { content: [{ text: "yo" }] }).content).toBe("yo");
    expect(parseResponse(PROVIDERS.google, { candidates: [{ content: { parts: [{ text: "hey" }] } }] }).content).toBe("hey");
  });
});

describe("registry (Day 6)", () => {
  it("resolves unknown providers to null", () => {
    expect(getProvider("nope")).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("insight prompts (Days 7/17)", () => {
  it("basic prompt caps findings and labels severity", () => {
    const findings = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => ({
      id: `dataops-${String(i).padStart(3, "0")}`,
      severity: "error",
      title: `t${i}`,
    }));
    const { prompt } = buildInsightPrompt(findings, "dataops");
    expect(prompt).toContain("40 total");
    expect(prompt).toContain("[ERROR] dataops-000");
  });

  it("grounded prompt degrades gracefully when no rules match", () => {
    const { groundedRuleIds, sources } = buildGroundedPrompt(
      [{ id: "iiot-999", severity: "critical", title: "x" }],
      "iiot"
    );
    // graph.json may not exist yet — must not throw, must return empty grounding
    expect(Array.isArray(groundedRuleIds)).toBe(true);
    expect(sources).toHaveProperty("concepts");
  });
});

describe("citation gate (Day 18)", () => {
  const opts = {
    validRuleIds: new Set(["iiot-001", "dataops-001"]),
    knownSources: KNOWN_SOURCES,
  };

  it("accepts rule IDs present in the graph", () => {
    const r = validateCitations("This violates rule iiot-001 badly.", opts);
    expect(r.grounded).toBe(true);
    expect(r.validCitations).toContain("iiot-001");
    expect(r.stripped).toBe(0);
  });

  it("flags rule IDs not present in the graph", () => {
    const r = validateCitations("See iiot-999 for details.", opts);
    expect(r.validCitations).not.toContain("iiot-999");
    expect(r.strippedCitations).toContain("iiot-999");
  });

  it("strips page refs in unsourced sentences", () => {
    const r = validateCitations("This is wrong, see page 40 for the proof.", opts);
    expect(r.text).toContain("[source unverified]");
    expect(r.stripped).toBe(1);
  });

  it("keeps page refs when the sentence cites a known book", () => {
    const r = validateCitations(
      "Industrial Network Security, page 40, requires network segmentation.",
      opts
    );
    expect(r.text).toContain("page 40");
    expect(r.stripped).toBe(0);
    expect(r.validCitations).toContain("industrial network security");
  });

  it("strips fabricated author citations", () => {
    const r = validateCitations("According to Smithfield this pattern is safe.", opts);
    expect(r.text).toContain("[source unverified]");
  });
});

===== FILE: __tests__/specs.test.ts =====
// __tests__/specs.test.ts — Week 1 acceptance: schema, uniqueness, references
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import yaml from "js-yaml";

const SPECS_DIR = path.join(process.cwd(), "docs", "specs");

interface SpecFile {
  version?: string;
  profile: string;
  rules: Array<Record<string, unknown>>;
}

function loadSpecFiles(): SpecFile[] {
  return fs
    .readdirSync(SPECS_DIR)
    .filter((f) => f.endsWith(".yaml") && !f.startsWith("_"))
    .map((f) => yaml.load(fs.readFileSync(path.join(SPECS_DIR, f), "utf-8")) as SpecFile);
}

const REQUIRED = ["id", "profile", "severity", "title", "rationale", "checks", "fix", "references"];
const SEVERITIES = ["critical", "error", "warning", "info"];

describe("spec manifests", () => {
  it("loads at least the three domain profiles + cross", () => {
    const files = loadSpecFiles();
    const profiles = files.map((f) => f.profile).sort();
    expect(profiles).toEqual(["dataops", "iiot", "webapp"]);
  });

  it("every rule has all required fields", () => {
    for (const file of loadSpecFiles()) {
      for (const rule of file.rules) {
        for (const field of REQUIRED) {
          expect(rule).toHaveProperty(field);
        }
      }
    }
  });

  it("rule ids are unique and convention-shaped", () => {
    const seen = new Set<string>();
    for (const file of loadSpecFiles()) {
      for (const rule of file.rules) {
        const id = String(rule.id);
        expect(seen.has(id)).toBe(false);
        expect(id).toMatch(/^(iiot|dataops|webapp|cross|general)(-[a-z0-9]+)*-\d{3}[a-z]?$/);
        seen.add(id);
      }
    }
  });

  it("every reference cites a book or a standard (never bare prose)", () => {
    for (const file of loadSpecFiles()) {
      for (const rule of file.rules) {
        const refs = rule.references as Array<Record<string, string>>;
        expect(Array.isArray(refs)).toBe(true);
        for (const ref of refs) {
          expect(Boolean(ref.book || ref.standard)).toBe(true);
          expect(typeof ref.concept).toBe("string");
        }
      }
    }
  });

  it("severities are from the valid set", () => {
    for (const file of loadSpecFiles()) {
      for (const rule of file.rules) {
        expect(SEVERITIES).toContain(rule.severity);
      }
    }
  });
});
===== END FILE: __tests__/specs.test.ts =====

===== FILE: __tests__/graph-grounding.test.ts =====
// __tests__/graph-grounding.test.ts — Day 20 acceptance tests (offline).
import { detectCrossDomain } from "@/lib/knowledge/graph";
import { validateCitations } from "@/lib/knowledge/citation-gate";
import { KNOWN_SOURCES } from "@/lib/knowledge/books";

describe("cross-domain detection (Day 19)", () => {
  it("flags multi-domain findings", () => {
    const r = detectCrossDomain([{ profile: "iiot" }, { profile: "dataops" }]);
    expect(r.crossDomain).toBe(true);
    expect(r.domains.sort()).toEqual(["dataops", "iiot"]);
  });

  it("single-domain findings are not cross-domain", () => {
    const r = detectCrossDomain([{ profile: "webapp" }, { profile: "webapp" }]);
    expect(r.crossDomain).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe("gate end-to-end (Day 20)", () => {
  const opts = { validRuleIds: new Set(["cross-001", "iiot-002"]), knownSources: KNOWN_SOURCES };

  it("a fully grounded answer passes untouched", () => {
    const text =
      "The primary risk is cross-001: shared dependency spaces. " +
      "Practical DataOps recommends environment parity here.";
    const r = validateCitations(text, opts);
    expect(r.grounded).toBe(true);
    expect(r.text).toBe(text);
    expect(r.stripped).toBe(0);
  });

  it("a fabricated 'Book X page 40' reference is stripped", () => {
    const r = validateCitations(
      "Use pattern Y, described in Book X page 40, immediately.",
      opts
    );
    expect(r.text).toContain("[source unverified]");
    expect(r.text).not.toContain("page 40");
  });
});

===== FILE: __tests__/graph-spec.test.ts =====
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import yaml from "js-yaml";

const SPECS_DIR = path.join(process.cwd(), "docs", "specs");

interface SpecFile {
  profile: string;
  rules: Array<{ id: string }>;
}

function loadGraphRuleIds(): Set<string> {
  const file = path.join(SPECS_DIR, "graph.yaml");
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return new Set();
  const data = yaml.load(fs.readFileSync(file, "utf-8")) as SpecFile;
  return new Set(data.rules.map((r) => r.id));
}

describe("graph spec ids", () => {
  it("graph.yaml ids follow convention", () => {
    const ids = [...loadGraphRuleIds()];
    for (const id of ids) {
      expect(id).toMatch(/^graph(-[a-z0-9]+)*-\d{3}[a-z]?$/);
    }
  });

  it("no duplicate graph ids", () => {
    const file = path.join(SPECS_DIR, "graph.yaml");
    if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return;
    const data = yaml.load(fs.readFileSync(file, "utf-8")) as SpecFile;
    const seen = new Set<string>();
    for (const rule of data.rules) {
      expect(seen.has(rule.id)).toBe(false);
      seen.add(rule.id);
    }
  });
});
===== END FILE: __tests__/graph-spec.test.ts =====

"""
RepoScope acceptance tests — verify the architectural claims made in the ADR,
the integrity of the docs/notes knowledge corpus, the deterministic three-layer
routing engine, and a live grounding round-trip against the local Ollama model.

Run from the repo root:

    # Offline suite (default — safe in CI, no model required)
    pytest __tests__/test_specs.py -v

    # Include live Ollama round-trips against the local Qwen model
    pytest __tests__/test_specs.py -v -m live

    # Everything
    pytest __tests__/test_specs.py -v -m "live or not live"

Design notes:
- Offline-first. The only network call is to localhost (Ollama), and only
  under the `live` marker.
- Path is anchored to this file so the same suite runs on Windows, macOS,
  Linux, and CI. Override with REPOSCOPE_ROOT.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import sys
import urllib.error
import urllib.request
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]

import pytest
import yaml

# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Marker registration (keeps `-m live` warning-free without pytest.ini)
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def pytest_configure(config: pytest.Config) -> None:
    config.addinivalue_line(
        "markers",
        "live: requires a running local Ollama server and the RepoScope model "
               "(deselected by default in CI; run with `-m live`)",
    )


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Paths
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
_ENV_ROOT = os.environ.get("REPOSCOPE_ROOT")
_WINDOWS_ROOT = Path(r"D:\Machine learning\NEXT.JS RepoScope\my-reposcope-app")
_FILE_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

if _ENV_ROOT:
    REPO_ROOT = Path(_ENV_ROOT)
elif _WINDOWS_ROOT.exists():
    REPO_ROOT = _WINDOWS_ROOT
else:
    REPO_ROOT = _FILE_ROOT

SPECS_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "docs" / "specs"
NOTES_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "docs" / "notes"
LIB_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "lib"
SCRIPTS_KG_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "scripts" / "kg"
KNOWLEDGE_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "public" / "knowledge"
KNOWLEDGE_FILE = KNOWLEDGE_DIR / "graph.json"
SPECS_JSON_FILE = KNOWLEDGE_DIR / "specs.json"
BOOKS_TS = LIB_DIR / "knowledge" / "books.ts"
TYPES_TS = LIB_DIR / "types.ts"

# External assets — overridable so CI and collaborators are unaffected.
OLLAMA_MODEL_DIR = Path(

OLLAMA_TIMEOUT = int(os.environ.get("REPOSCOPE_OLLAMA_TIMEOUT", "240"))

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Session-scoped fixtures (fix for issue #8 — no repeated disk reads)
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def yaml_specs() -> dict[Path, dict]:
    """{yaml_path: parsed_content} for every non-underscore spec manifest."""
    specs: dict[Path, dict] = {}
    if not SPECS_DIR.is_dir():
        return specs
    for path in sorted(SPECS_DIR.glob("*.yaml")) + sorted(SPECS_DIR.glob("*.yml")):
        if path.name.startswith("_"):
            continue
        try:
            specs[path] = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) or {}
        except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
            pytest.fail(f"YAML parse error in {path.name}: {exc}")
    return specs


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def yaml_rule_ids(yaml_specs: dict[Path, dict]) -> set[str]:
    ids: set[str] = set()
    for doc in yaml_specs.values():
        for rule in doc.get("rules", []) or []:
            rid = rule.get("id")
            if rid:
                ids.add(str(rid).strip())
    return ids


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def ts_rule_ids() -> set[str]:
    """Every `ruleId: '...'` literal in the TS emitter files."""
    ids: set[str] = set()
    pattern = re.compile(r"ruleId\s*:\s*['\"]([^'\"]+)['\"]")
    for src in TS_EMITTERS:
        if not src.exists():
            continue
        for match in pattern.finditer(src.read_text(encoding="utf-8")):
            ids.add(match.group(1).strip())
    return ids


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def note_files() -> dict[str, list[Path]]:
    """{top-level folder: [markdown paths]} under docs/notes."""
    out: dict[str, list[Path]] = {}
    if not NOTES_DIR.is_dir():
        return out
    for md in sorted(NOTES_DIR.rglob("*.md")):
        rel = md.relative_to(NOTES_DIR)
        if len(rel.parts) < 2:          # skip docs/notes/README.md
            continue
        out.setdefault(rel.parts[0], []).append(md)
    return out


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def repo_signals():
    """One deterministic scan of the RepoScope repository, reused everywhere."""
    try:
        from route_knowledge import scan_repo
    except ImportError:
        pytest.skip("scripts/kg/route_knowledge.py not importable.")
    return scan_repo(REPO_ROOT)


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def routed_bundle(repo_signals):
    try:
        from route_knowledge import route
    except ImportError:
        pytest.skip("scripts/kg/route_knowledge.py not importable.")
    return route(REPO_ROOT, signals=repo_signals)


# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Ollama helpers (live tests only)
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
def _ollama_get(path: str, timeout: int = 5):
    url = f"{OLLAMA_HOST}{path}"
    req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": "RepoScope-Test"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as resp:
        return resp.status, json.loads(resp.read().decode("utf-8"))


def _ollama_available() -> bool:
    try:
        status, _ = _ollama_get("/api/tags")
        return status == 200
    except Exception:
        return False


def _ollama_model_names() -> list[str]:
    try:
        _, data = _ollama_get("/api/tags")
    except Exception:
        return []
    return [m.get("name", "") for m in data.get("models", [])]


def _resolve_model() -> str | None:
    """
    Resolve the model name to call, in priority order:
      1. REPOSCOPE_OLLAMA_MODEL env var (explicit)
      2. an exact `reposcope-qwen*` registration
      3. any registered model matching the Qwen3.5-9B family
      4. any Qwen-family model
    """
    if OLLAMA_MODEL_HINT:
        return OLLAMA_MODEL_HINT

    names = _ollama_model_names()
    if not names:
        return None

    def _base(n: str) -> str:
        return n.split(":")[0].lower()

    for n in names:
        if _base(n).startswith("reposcope-qwen"):
            return n
    for n in names:
        b = _base(n)
        if "qwen" in b and ("9b" in b or "3.5" in b or "q4_k_m" in b):
            return n
    for n in names:
        if "qwen" in _base(n):
            return n
    return None


def _ollama_generate(model: str, prompt: str, system: str = "",
                     temperature: float = 0.1, num_predict: int = 512) -> str:
    """Blocking generation call against the local Ollama server."""
    payload = json.dumps({
        "model": model,
        "prompt": prompt,
        "system": system,
        "stream": False,
        "options": {
            "temperature": temperature,
            "num_predict": num_predict,
        },
    }).encode("utf-8")
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        f"{OLLAMA_HOST}/api/generate",
        data=payload,
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json", "User-Agent": "RepoScope-Test"},
        method="POST",
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=OLLAMA_TIMEOUT) as resp:
        data = json.loads(resp.read().decode("utf-8"))
    return str(data.get("response", "")).strip()


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def ollama_model() -> str:
    """Session fixture: the resolved local model name, or skip."""
    if not _ollama_available():
        pytest.skip(
            f"Ollama server not reachable at {OLLAMA_HOST}. "
            "Start it with: ollama serve"
        )
    model = _resolve_model()
    if not model:
        pytest.skip(
            f"No Qwen-family model registered on Ollama. Found: {_ollama_model_names()}. "
            "Register the local GGUF with:\n"
            "  cd 'D:\\Machine learning\\Ollama Models\\Qwen_Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M'\n"
            "  ollama create reposcope-qwen -f Modelfile"
        )
    return model
